(44)

This leads to the composite morphism
\mathcal{O}_{\omega}
shown in the center. Its right adjoint
\mathcal{O}_{\omega}^{\flat}
is that element of
\PathF(\Omega)
which corresponds to the path traced out by
\omega
.

Although our construction of the path-space object
\PathF(\Omega)
has been categorical, it contains all the information one would associate to the classical definition of a path-space. Unlike a set-theoretic description, this information is encoded not within
\PathF(\Omega)
but in its hom-sets. For example, the time-0 evaluation which associates to every path its current state, is created as the following composite morphism :

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1_{\Context}\otimes\PathF(\Omega)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\PathF(\Omega)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\PathF(\Omega)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta\otimes\PathF(\Omega)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.3672,0.0742}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{eval}_{0}}
eval

(45)

The time-0 evaluation can be combined with the natural flow of the path to get the time-t evaluation operation :

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1_{\Context}\otimes\PathF(\Omega)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\PathF(\Omega)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\PathF(\Omega)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\PathF(\Omega)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\PathF(\Omega)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1_{\Context}\otimes\PathF(\Omega)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta\otimes\PathF(\Omega)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{eval}_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\shift_{\Omega}}
eval
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.3672,0.0742}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{eval}_{t}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t\otimes\PathF(\Omega)}

(46)

This completes our presentation of a categorical language for dynamical systems that completely captures all different aspects of dynamics - as a flow, as a semigroup action, as a parameterized family of endomorphisms, and as a subshift of the path-space. We have proposed that dynamical systems be studied as enriched functors and not simply as functors of a semigroup into the semigroup of endomorphisms of a domain
\Omega
. This interpretation is enabled by four categorical Assumptions
1
,
2
,
3
and
4
.
The preliminary notions of a dynamical system, such as states, invariance and paths occur naturally as by-products. Theorem
7
shows how this makes the three descriptions of dynamical systems interchangeable. This leads to the concept of an states, stationary enriched states, and stationary states. Theorem
7
and the notion of states lead to the notion of an orbit as an element of the path space of an object.

This work aims to provides the platform for a deeper investigation into dynamics, using a completely categorical language. In our final section, we look at some further challenges to applying this framework to the classical contexts in dynamical systems theory.

7  Conclusions

As mentioned earlier, the various contexts in dynamical systems usually have the neat structure of a functor category. This is illustrated by the following chain of monoidal categories :

Vec

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textbf{ \text{Man}}^{r}}

Topo

Meas

Stoch

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota^{\prime\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota^{\prime\prime\prime}}

(47)

The four context categories chosen in this diagram satisfy Assumption
2
and
3
. If time
\mathcal{T}
is chosen to be a topological semigroup such as
\left\llbracket\mathbb{N}\uparrow\right\rrbracket
,
\mathbb{Z}
or then Assumptions
1
and
2
would also be satisfied. The sequence of inclusions imply the fact that linear spaces and transformations are also smooth spaces and transformations; smooth spaces and transformations are topological spaces and continuous transformations; continuous transformations are Borel-measurable spaces and measurable transformations; and the latter are measurable spaces and Markov transitions. The last link is based on the realization that a deterministic map
f:X\to Y
between two spaces is also a Markov transition, associating the point-measure
\delta_{f(x)}
to each point
x\in X
.
The diagram (
47
) give the following chain of dynamical systems

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{array}[]{c}\mbox{Linear}\\
\mbox{dynamics}\\
\mathbb{F}\left(\mathcal{T}\;;\;\textbf{ \text{Vec} }\right)\end{array}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{array}[]{c}C^{r}-\mbox{smooth}\\
\mbox{dynamics}\\
\mathbb{F}\left(\mathcal{T}\;;\;\textbf{ \text{Man}}^{r}\right)\end{array}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{array}[]{c}\mbox{Topological}\\
\mbox{dynamics}\\
\mathbb{F}\left(\mathcal{T}\;;\;\textbf{ \text{Topo} }\right)\end{array}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{array}[]{c}\mbox{Measurable}\\
\mbox{dynamics}\\
\mathbb{F}\left(\mathcal{T}\;;\;\textbf{ \text{Meas} }\right)\end{array}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{array}[]{c}\mbox{Markov}\\
\mbox{process}\\
\mathbb{F}\left(\mathcal{T}\;;\;\textbf{ \text{Stoch} }\right)\end{array}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota\circ}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota^{\prime}\circ}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota^{\prime\prime}\circ}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota^{\prime\prime\prime}\circ}

(48)

The diagram in (
48
) is useful as it formally states an inclusion of categories of dynamical systems in various categories. However this diagram only involves ordinary functor categories, and not enriched functor categories. So although intuitive and comprehensive, our theoretical results on enriched categories do not apply to these functors. Ideally, the dynamics in two different contexts should be expressed as enriched functor categories connected via enriched functors. There are two main challenges to this. First is the nature of the context category itself. The categories

Topo

,

Meas

and

Stoch

are monoidal but not closed. There are many alternatives to these classical categories :

1.

the category

CGWHS
of
compactly generated, weakly generated Hausdorff spaces

Steenrod1967cnvnnt
is a Cartesian closed subcategory of

Topo

.

2.

the category

QuasiBorel

HeunenEtAl2017cnvnt
of
quasi-Borel spaces
is a Cartesian closed subcategory of

Meas

.

3.

the category

Diffeology
of
diffeological spaces

Stacey2008smooth
;
BaezHoffnung2011cnvnnt
is a Cartesian closed subcategory of
\textbf{ \text{Man}}^{1}
. Another alternative to
\textbf{ \text{Man}}^{1}
are Chen spaces
Chen1977iterated
.

An important task ahead is to develop the theory of enriched
\mathcal{T}
-domain functors on these closed categories, and relate them to contexts they are meant to replace.

A field where a categorical formulation of dynamics can have a major impact is in the study of numerical methods for reconstructing dynamical systems. This field was recently placed in a categorical footing by the authors in
DasSuda2024recon
. The entire process of reconstruction was presented as an inter-play between four different categories – observed dynamical systems, dynamical systems, timeseries data, and subshifts. Reconstructions were shown to be closely related to left adjoints. One of the classic results in ergodic theory is that any finite timeseries can be created from Bernoulli processes as well as deterministic processes. To study the convergence of reconstruction algorithms, one needs to be cognizant of the marginal distributions of the source of the data. This requires incorporating a categorical version of the Kolmogorov consistency theorem
(
FritzRischel2020inf
;
vanBelle2023mrtngl
, e.g.)
using the language of
filtered colimits

(
AndrekaNemeti1982direct
, e.g.)
.

Developing dynamical systems theory in a categorical language also requires new proofs of many of the classical results in this theory. An important example is a categorical proof of the ergodic decomposition theorem
MossPerrone2022ergdc
. An important and unfinished task is show that the infinitely and densely nested character of chaotic, ergodic systems is a natural consequence of underlying categorical assumption. While the notion of nesting corresponds to morphisms and chains of morphisms in
\mathbb{F}\left(\mathcal{T}\;;\;\textbf{ \text{Topo} }\right)
, the concept of density is a harder concept to capture using categorical language
(
ClementinoTholen1997sep
;
ClementinoGiuliTholen1996
, e.g.)
.

Developing a categorical restatement of dynamical systems theory is a promising venture, and there still remain several challenges such as these.

T.S. is grateful to the Grants-in-Aid for Scientific Research (Kakenhi) 25K17284.

9  Appendix

9.1  Some technical lemmas

Lemma 9.1
.

Suppose Assumptions
2
and
3
hold. Consider a morphism of the form
\phi:\mathbb{T}\otimes X\to\Omega
and its right-adjoint
\phi^{*}:X\to\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}
. Then one has the following commutation

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\phi^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{eval}_{\Omega}}

(49)

Lemma
9.1
is a direct consequence of the fact that the evaluation morphism corresponds to the counit of the product-inner product adjunction of
\Context
. Lemma
9.1
provides a formulation for left-adjoint
{}^{*}\gamma
of a morphisms
\gamma
with co-domain
\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\gamma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{*}\gamma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{eval}_{\Omega}}

(50)

Thus the left adjoints of morphisms with co-domain
\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}
can be obtained by composition with the evaluation morphism. Two special cases of Lemma
9.1
are important to us. The first is the following instance of
\tilde{\Phi}
:

X:=\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}},\quad\phi:=\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}\xrightarrow{\add\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}\xrightarrow{\text{eval}_{\Omega}}\Omega.

Recall that the adjoint of this morphism is the
\shift
-morphism. Then the commutation in (
49
) takes the form :

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\shift}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\add\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{eval}_{\Omega}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{eval}_{\Omega}}

(51)

9.1
assumes

9.2  Results on enriched category theory

The purpose of this section is to examine the theoretical tools needed to switch back and forth between enriched semigroups, monoids, enriched functors and flows. We begin with an important observation that
\mathbb{T}
itself is a familiar categorical construct.

Lemma 9.2
.

Let
\mathcal{T}
be a semigroup enriched over a monoidal category
\Context
. Then the objects and morphisms
\left(\mathbb{T},\add,\eta\right)
defined in Section
3
form a monoid object.

The lemma directly follows from the definition and identities in (
3
) and (
4
). The converse also happens to be true, that a monoid object leads to a semigroup enriched over
\Context
:

Lemma 9.3
.

Let Assumptions
2
and
3
hold. Let
\left(\mathbb{T},\add:\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\to\mathbb{T},\eta:1_{\Context}\to T\right)
be a monoid in
\Context
. Then we can define a one-object category
\mathcal{T}_{T}
enriched over
\Context
as follows :

1.

The only object is denoted as
*
.

2.

The morphisms correspond to the
\Context
-morphisms
1_{\Context}\to T
. The composition
a\cdot b
of two morphisms
a
and
b
is defined by

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1_{\Context}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1_{\Context}\otimes 1_{\Context}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a\cdot b}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b\otimes a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\add}

3.

The identity morphism is given by
\eta
.

The next lemma formally states an intuitive fact, that the enriched semigroup created from a monoid object which itself is created out of an enriched semigroup, coincides with the original semigroup.

Lemma 9.4
.

Let Assumptions
1
,
2
and
3
hold. Let
\mathbb{T}
be the
\Context
-object created from
\mathcal{T}
by enrichment, and
\mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{T}}
be the semigroup created from
\mathbb{T}
according to Lemma
9.3
. Then
\mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{T}}
coincides with
\mathcal{T}
.

In other words the constructions Lemma
9.2
and Lemma
9.3
are inverses of each other. Having established the dual nature of enriched semigroups, we turn to Lemma
4.2
.

Proof of Lemma
4.2

Claim (i) follows directly from the discussion preceding the theorem. We prove the Claim (ii). We begin with the ”if” part of the statement. So we assume that there is an enriched functor
\tilde{\psi}
, whose parametric form is
\psi^{\sharp}
, and
\psi
is its left adjoint. Now consider the following diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1_{\Context}\otimes\Omega}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\Omega}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left[\Omega\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}\otimes\Omega}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{*}\otimes\Omega}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{\Omega}\otimes\Omega}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda_{\Omega}^{(l)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psi^{\sharp}\otimes\Omega}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{eval}_{\Omega}}

(55)

The commutation in the periphery is just the unit law of the evaluation morphism. The lower commutation triangle is the adjoint relation of Lemma
9.1
. The left commutation triangle follows from (
22
). The right commutation triangle is a composite of these three commutations. It also happens to be the unit law in (
23
). Next consider the diagram :

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\Omega}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\Omega}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\Omega\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}\otimes\Omega}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left[\Omega\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}\otimes\left[\Omega\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}\otimes\Omega}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left[\Omega\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}\otimes\Omega}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left[\Omega\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}\otimes\Omega}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\Omega}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\Psi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\Psi^{\sharp}\otimes\Omega}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\add\otimes\Omega}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psi^{\sharp}\otimes\Omega}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{eval}_{\Omega}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\text{eval}_{\Omega}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psi^{\sharp}\otimes\left[\Omega\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}\otimes\Omega}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left[\Omega\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}\otimes\text{eval}_{\Omega}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\circ\otimes\Omega}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{eval}_{\Omega}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{eval}_{\Omega}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psi^{\sharp}\otimes\Omega}

(56)

Each of the commutation loops in this diagram are instances of the adjunction in Lemma
9.1
. The commutation on the periphery is the composite of all the minimal commutation loops. It expresses the composition law of (
23
). This completes the proof of the ”if” part.

The ”only if” part also follows a study of the commutation diagrams (
55
) and (
56
) above. In this case, the smaller commutation loops which are assumed and which need to be proved switch roles. This completes the proof of Lemma
4.2
. ∎

Lemma
4.2
thus establishes an equivalence of left
\mathbb{T}
actions and enriched functors. An left action can be created not only out of an enriched semigroup, but also from a monoid object. We next see that the latter also leads to an enriched semigroup action :

Lemma 9.5
.

Let
\Context
be a closed monoidal category and
\left(\mathbb{T},\add:\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\to\mathbb{T},\eta:\Context\to\mathbb{T}\right)
be a monoid of
\Context
. If
F:\mathbb{T}\otimes\Omega\to\Omega
is a left
\mathbb{T}
-action, then the following construction defines a functor
\mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{T}}\to\Context
enriched over
\Context
.

•

The object
*
corresponds to
\Omega
.

•

A morphism
a:1_{\Context}\to\mathbb{T}
in
\mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{T}}
corresponds to

\Omega\to 1_{\Context}\otimes\Omega\xrightarrow{a\otimes\Omega}\mathbb{T}\otimes\Omega\xrightarrow{F}\Omega.

We end with an observation that an enriched functor can be reconstructed by the construction of Lemma
9.5
respectively.

Lemma 9.6
.

Let
\Context
be a symmetric closed monoidal category and
\mathcal{T}
be a one-object category enriched over
\Context
. Assume that a functor
F:\mathcal{T}\to\Context
is an enriched functor. Then,
F
coincides with the enriched functor obtained from a consecutive application of Lemma
4.2
and Lemma
9.5
to
F
.

9.3  Enriched functors from semigroups

The purpose of this short section is to review the conditions for being enriched functors and enriched natural transformations, when the the domain is a semigroup
\mathcal{T}
enriched over the codomain
\Context
. Thus according to the notation of Section
4
this means
\mathcal{Y}=\mathcal{M}=\Context
and
\mathcal{X}=\mathcal{T}
. In that case (
20
) has only one morphism

\omega_{*}:1_{\Context}\to\left[\Omega\,;\,\Omega^{\prime}\right]_{\text{int}}

(57)

and (
21
) becomes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes 1_{\mathcal{C}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Endo(\Omega^{\prime})\otimes\left[\Omega\,;\,\Omega^{\prime}\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left[\Omega\,;\,\Omega^{\prime}\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1_{\mathcal{C}}\otimes\mathbb{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left[\Omega\,;\,\Omega^{\prime}\right]_{\text{int}}\otimes\Endo(\Omega)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G^{\sharp}\otimes\omega_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\circ}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\omega}_{*}\otimes F^{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\circ}

(58)

We first verify the unit law. Let
\lambda^{(L)}
and
\lambda^{(R)}
denote the isomorphisms created by multiplication with
1_{\Context}
on the left and right respectively. Then we have the following diagram :

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes 1_{\Context}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\lambda^{(R)-1}_{[T;\Omega]}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda^{(L)}_{[T;\Omega]}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\eta_{*}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\add\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\shift}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{eval}_{\Omega}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{eval}_{\Omega}}

The commuting square has already been derived in (
51
). The two commuting triangles are trivial consequences of the unitor morphisms. Now note that the clockwise (CW) path from
\mathbb{T}\otimes 1_{\Context}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}
to
\Omega
is the left-adjoint of the counter-clockwise (CCW) path in the unit rule. This path is isomorphic to the CCW path from
\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}
to
\Omega
, which is a left-adjoint to
\lambda_{[T;\Omega]}^{(L)-1}
. Thus we have proved the commutation of the left-adjoints of the paths in the unit rule.

We next prove the second diagram. We shall show that the left adjoints to the two paths in the diagram are equal. These left adjoints have already been determined in (
53
) and (
54
) respectively. We shall need two identities for the proof. The first is a trivial application of the associativity of
\add
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\add\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\add\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\add\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\shift}

(59)

The second is a trivial application of the bifunctoriality of
\otimes
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\add\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\shift}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\add\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\shift}

(60)

Three repeated applications of (
51
) gives :

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\add\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\shift}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{eval}_{\Omega}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\add\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\shift}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{eval}_{\Omega}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\shift}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\add\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{eval}_{\Omega}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{eval}_{\Omega}}

Adding the commutations of (
59
) and (
60
) gives :

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\add\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\add\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\shift}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\add\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\shift}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{eval}_{\Omega}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\add\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\shift}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{eval}_{\Omega}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\shift}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\add\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{eval}_{\Omega}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{eval}_{\Omega}}

(61)

The resulting commutation in the peripheral loop of (
61
) confirms the equality of (
53
) and (
54
). This completes the proof of
6
. ∎

9.5  Proof of Theorem
7

The verification of compositionality and associativity in the two categories in claim (i) is routine and will be ommitted. The bijection between the objects claimed in statement (iii) have already been proved in Lemma
4.2
. Statements (iv) and (v) follow directly from (i), (ii) and (iii). Thus it remains to prove Claim (ii). This requires establishing a correspondence between morphisms in these two categories, that also preserves composition.

We will set up a functor
F:\left[\text{Left-action}\left(\Context\right)\right]\to\mathbb{F}_{\text{enrich}}\left(\mathcal{T};\Context\right)
by

•

\Phi\mapsto\tilde{\Phi}
on objects.

•

(h:\Phi\to\Psi)\mapsto(h^{\sharp}:\tilde{\Phi}\to\tilde{\Psi})
on morphisms.

To verify
F
is well-defined, we need the following lemma, which implies that a morphism
h:\Omega\to\Omega^{\prime}
in
\Context
is a morphism
h:\Phi\to\Psi
in
\left[\text{Left-action}\left(\Context\right)\right]
if and only if
h^{\sharp}:1_{\mathcal{C}}\to\left[\Omega\,;\,\Omega^{\prime}\right]_{\text{int}}
defines a morphism
\tilde{\Phi}\to\tilde{\Psi}
in
\mathbb{F}_{\text{enrich}}\left(\mathcal{T};\Context\right)
:

Lemma 9.7
.

Let
\Phi:\mathbb{T}\otimes\Omega\to\Omega
and
\Phi^{\prime}:\mathbb{T}\otimes\Omega^{\prime}\to\Omega^{\prime}
be morphisms in a context category
\mathcal{C}
. If
h:\Omega\to\Omega^{\prime}
and
h^{\sharp}:1_{\mathcal{C}}\to\left[\Omega\,;\,\Omega^{\prime}\right]_{\text{int}}
are adjoints, then the morphism

\mathbb{T}\otimes\Omega\xrightarrow{\Phi}\Omega\xrightarrow{h}\Omega^{\prime}

is adjoint to

\mathbb{T}\simeq 1_{\mathcal{C}}\otimes\mathbb{T}\xrightarrow{h^{\sharp}\otimes\Phi^{\sharp}}\left[\Omega\,;\,\Omega^{\prime}\right]_{\text{int}}\otimes\Endo{\Omega}\xrightarrow{\circ}\left[\Omega\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}.

Similarly, the morphism

\mathbb{T}\otimes\Omega\xrightarrow{\mathbb{T}\otimes h}\mathbb{T}\otimes\Omega^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\Phi^{\prime}}\Omega^{\prime}

is adjoint to

\mathbb{T}\simeq\mathbb{T}\otimes 1_{\mathcal{C}}\xrightarrow{\Phi^{\prime\sharp}\otimes h^{\sharp}}\Endo{\Omega^{\prime}}\otimes\left[\Omega\,;\,\Omega^{\prime}\right]_{\text{int}}\xrightarrow{\circ}\left[\Omega\,;\,\Omega^{\prime}\right]_{\text{int}}.

By the commutation in (
25
), we have

F(h^{\prime}\circ h)=F(h^{\prime})\circ F(h).

Thus,
F
is a functor and it is clear that
F
has inverse.
This completes the proof of Theorem
7
. ∎

We begin with the second diagram. We shall show that the adjoint to the two paths in the diagram are equal. By another use of Lemma
9.1
and (
51
), these adjoints are :

\displaystyle\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}\xrightarrow{\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\text{Tr}_{\Phi}}\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}\xrightarrow{\mathbb{T}\otimes\text{Tr}_{\Phi}}\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}\xrightarrow{\text{eval}_{\Omega}}\Omega

(62)

\displaystyle\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}\xrightarrow{\mathbb{T}\otimes\mu\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}\xrightarrow{\mathbb{T}\otimes\text{Tr}_{\Phi}}\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}\xrightarrow{\text{eval}_{\Omega}}\Omega

We shall need the following three identities in the proof :

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\Omega}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\Omega}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\text{Tr}_{\Phi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\text{eval}_{\Omega}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{swap}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\Phi^{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\text{eval}_{\Omega}}

(63)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\Omega}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\Omega}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\add\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\text{eval}_{\Omega}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\add\otimes\Omega}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{swap}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\text{eval}_{\Omega}}

(64)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\Omega}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\text{Tr}_{\Phi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{swap}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\text{eval}_{\Omega}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{eval}_{\Omega}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi}

(65)

The commutations in (
51
), (
52
) and (
63
) combine to give

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\Omega}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\Omega}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\Omega}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\text{Tr}_{\Phi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\text{eval}_{\Omega}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{swap}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\text{Tr}_{\Phi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\add\otimes\Omega}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\Phi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\text{eval}_{\Omega}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{eval}_{\Omega}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi}

The commutation in (
64
) can now be added to get

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\Omega}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\Omega}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\Omega}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\add\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\text{Tr}_{\Phi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\text{eval}_{\Omega}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{swap}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\text{Tr}_{\Phi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\add\otimes\Omega}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\Phi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\text{eval}_{\Omega}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{eval}_{\Omega}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\text{eval}_{\Omega}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{swap}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}

The commutation in (
65
) can now be added to get the large diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\Omega}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\Omega}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\Omega}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\add\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\text{Tr}_{\Phi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\text{eval}_{\Omega}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{swap}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\text{Tr}_{\Phi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\add\otimes\Omega}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\Phi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\text{eval}_{\Omega}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{eval}_{\Omega}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\text{eval}_{\Omega}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{swap}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\text{Tr}_{\Phi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{eval}_{\Omega}}

(66)

Note that the outer periphery of this diagram reflects the equality of the two paths in (
62
).

As for the unit law, we have the following diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{T}\otimes 1_{\Context}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{1_{\Context}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1_{\Context}\otimes\Omega}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\Omega}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\lambda}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{swap}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\eta\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{swap}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\text{Tr}_{\Phi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{eval}_{\Omega}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1_{\Context}\otimes\text{eval}_{\Omega}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\text{eval}_{\Omega}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta\otimes\Omega}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{eval}_{\Omega}}

The outermost clockwise path from
\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}
to
\Omega
is the left adjoint of the transfer operator
\text{Tr}_{\Phi}
by Lemma
9.1
. The counter clockwise path from
\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}
to
\Omega
is the also left adjoint of
\text{Tr}_{\Phi}
from the definition (
14
). This explains the top-right commuting square. All the other commuting triangles and squares follow directly from the consistency properties of
\eta
and
swap
. Now note two peripheral paths in this diagram are exactly the left adjoints of the two paths in the unit law identity, again by Lemma
9.1
. This completes the proof of Theorem
9
. ∎

9.7  Proof of Theorem
10

Our first observation is that the following diagram commutes.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G}

(67)

To see why we expand the above diagram into :

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,X\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes F^{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes G^{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G}
eval

The top commutation follows from the equalizer property of
q
. The two commuting loops on the right follow from (
50
). This completes the proof of (
67
).

The next important observation is the following commutation

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,X\right]_{\text{int}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F^{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G^{\sharp}}

(68)

The first commutation is already covered in (
67
). We thus need to show that the following two paths have the same adjoints :

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,X\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,X\right]_{\text{int}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F^{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G^{\sharp}}

Call these paths
\tilde{F}
and
\tilde{G}
respectively. Again by (
50
), it is equivalent to show that

\text{eval}\circ\left(\mathbb{T}\otimes\tilde{F}\right)=\text{eval}\circ\left(\mathbb{T}\otimes\tilde{G}\right).

This follows from the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,X\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,X\right]_{\text{int}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\add\otimes E}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\add\otimes X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes F^{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
eval
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\add\otimes E}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\add\otimes X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes G}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes G^{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G}
eval

This completes the proof of (
68
).

The commutation in (
68
) along with the universality of the equalizer
q
implies the existence of a unique morphism shown below as a dotted arrow

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces!}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}

This morphism is precisely the morphism
F\cap G
. Note that the commutation in (
30
) is automatically borne by the figure above.

We next prove the second claim. To show that
F\cap G
is a left action, we first verify its compositionality.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\add\otimes X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes G}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\add\otimes E}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes F\cap G}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\cap G}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\cap G}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G}

In the diagram above all the commuting loops not involving the dotted arrow has already been established. The dotted arrow exists uniquely by the equalizer property of
q
. The unit law follows from the following diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E_{\Phi}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1_{\Context}\otimes E_{\Phi}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes E_{\Phi}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E_{\Phi}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1_{\Context}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda_{E_{\Phi}}^{(L)-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1_{\Context}\otimes q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{*}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\shift_{\Phi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda_{[T,\Omega]}^{(L)-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{*}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\shift}

This completes the proof of Theorem
10
. ∎

9.8  Proof of Theorem
11

The first four claims of the theorem follow directly from Theorem
10
. It thus remains to prove Claim (v). We need two lemmas.

Lemma 9.8
.

The construction
Tr
defines an endomorphism of the category of left
\mathbb{T}
-actions.

Proof.

Let
f:\Phi\to\Psi
be a morphism of left
\mathbb{T}
-actions, with domains
\Omega,\Omega^{\prime}
respectively. We need to show the following commutation :

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega^{\prime}\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega^{\prime}\right]_{\text{int}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{Tr}_{\Phi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,f\right]_{\text{int}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{Tr}_{\Phi^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,f\right]_{\text{int}}}

The following commutation diagram establishes the equality of the left adjoints of these two morphisms :

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\Omega}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes[\mathbb{T};\Omega^{\prime}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes[\mathbb{T};\Omega^{\prime}]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\Omega^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{T}\otimes[\mathbb{T};\Omega^{\prime}]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega^{\prime}\right]_{\text{int}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,f\right]_{\text{int}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces swap\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega^{\prime}\right]_{\text{int}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\text{Tr}_{\Phi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,f\right]_{\text{int}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\text{eval}_{\Omega}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{eval}_{\Omega}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,f\right]_{\text{int}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\text{Tr}_{\Phi^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces swap\otimes[\mathbb{T};\Omega^{\prime}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\text{eval}_{\Omega^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\Phi^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{eval}_{\Omega^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{eval}_{\Omega^{\prime}}}

The two paths along the periphery from
\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}
to
\Omega^{\prime}
are the left adjoints of the two paths in the commutative diagram to be proved. The commutation loop at the top is an application of Lemma
9.1
and the definition (
14
) of the transfer operator. Ever other commutation follows from bi-functoriality.
∎

Now, the proof proceeds as follows. Lemmas
9.8
and (
27
) together imply that the following morphisms have the same adjoint and therefore coincide:

E_{\Phi}\xrightarrow{q}\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}\xrightarrow{\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,f\right]_{\text{int}}}\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega^{\prime}\right]_{\text{int}}\xrightarrow[\text{Tr}_{\Psi}^{\flat}]{\shift^{\flat}}\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega^{\prime}\right]_{\text{int}}\right]_{\text{int}}

Then, the left commutative diagram in (
34
) follows immediately from the universal property of the equalizer.

For the second commutative diagram in (
34
), by a similar argument, we can also verify that the following morphisms coincide:

\mathbb{T}\otimes E_{\Phi}\xrightarrow{\mathbb{T}\otimes E(f)}\mathbb{T}\otimes E_{\Psi}\xrightarrow{\shift_{\Psi}}E_{\Psi}\xrightarrow{q^{\prime}}\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega^{\prime}\right]_{\text{int}}

\mathbb{T}\otimes E_{\Phi}\xrightarrow{\shift_{\Phi}}E_{\Phi}\xrightarrow{E(f)}E_{\Psi}\xrightarrow{q^{\prime}}\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega^{\prime}\right]_{\text{int}}

Again by the universal property of the equalizer, we obtain the desired diagram.
This completes the proof of Theorem
11
. ∎

9.9   Proof of Equation (
27
)

In a manner analogous of the proof of Lemma
9.8
the proof follows from the following commutative diagram :

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega^{\prime}\right]_{\text{int}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega^{\prime}\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega^{\prime}\right]_{\text{int}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega^{\prime}\right]_{\text{int}}}}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.3672,0.0742}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{eval}_{\Omega^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{eval}_{\Omega^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,f\right]_{\text{int}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{eval}_{\Omega}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega^{\prime}\right]_{\text{int}}}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.3672,0.0742}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\shift_{\Omega^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.3672,0.0742}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,f\right]_{\text{int}}}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.5664,0.1484,0.6523}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\shift_{\Omega}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.5664,0.1484,0.6523}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,f\right]_{\text{int}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{eval}_{\Omega}}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.5664,0.1484,0.6523}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{eval}_{\Omega^{\prime}}}

The rightmost commutation square follows from the naturality of the evaluation morphisms, the one in the middle follows from the flow property of
\shift
, and the one on the left follows from bi-functoriality of monoidal product. The commutation square at the top left corner is simply (
51
). The commutation square at the top with one vertex at
\Omega^{\prime}
also follows from the naturality of
eval
.
The CCW path in green, and the CW path in purple are the left adjoints to the CW and CCW paths respectively in (
27
), thus proving the commutation. ∎

9.10  Proof of Theorem
12

The proof proceeds in two steps. First we show that

\shift^{\flat}\circ\Phi^{\flat}=\text{Tr}_{\Phi}^{\flat}\circ\Phi^{\flat}

(69)

We prove (
69
) by proving that the double left adjoints (d.l.a) of the the two sides of the equation are equal. The d.l.a of the LHS of (
69
) is the clockwise path shown below :

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\Omega}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T\otimes T\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\Omega}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\otimes T\otimes\Phi^{\flat}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu\otimes\Omega}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\Phi^{\flat}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{eval}_{\Omega}}

(70)

The upper commutation square follows from the bi-functoriality of
\otimes
. The lower commutation triangle follows from (
49
). Overall, we get an equivalent expression for the d.l.a of the LHS of (
69
). We next study the d.l.a. of the RHS of (

). It is given by the clockwise path in the diagram below :

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\Omega}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T\otimes T\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\Omega}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{T}\otimes T\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\otimes\Omega}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\otimes\Omega}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\otimes T\otimes\Phi^{\flat}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{swap}\otimes\Omega}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu\otimes\Omega}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{swap}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes T\otimes\Phi^{\flat}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\otimes\Phi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu\otimes\Omega}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\text{eval}_{\Omega}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi}

(71)

Diagrams (

) and (
71
) establish the identity in (
69
). We have thus proven that
\Phi^{\flat}
equalizes
\shift^{\flat}
and
\text{Tr}_{\Phi}^{\flat}
. We next prove that it is fact the equalizer. So we assume a commutation of the form

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[T,\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}]}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\shift^{\flat}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{Tr}_{\Phi}}

and show that
\alpha
must factorize uniquely through
\Phi^{\flat}
. Define

as

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1_{\Context}\otimes A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{*}\otimes A}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{eval}_{\Omega}}

To verify that
\alpha=\Phi^{\flat}\circ\tilde{\alpha}
, we need the following commutation diagram :

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\Omega}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\otimes T\otimes\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbb{T}\otimes\text{eval})\circ(\text{swap}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{eval}_{\Omega}}

Using this commutativity, we obtain the following diagram, which verifies the desired factorization of
\alpha
by comparing their adjoints :

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes 1_{\Context}\otimes A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T\otimes T\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1_{\Context}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\Omega}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda_{\mathbb{T}}^{(R)-1}\otimes A}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda_{\mathbb{T}}^{(L)-1}\otimes A}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\eta_{*}\otimes A}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{swap}\otimes A}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{swap}\otimes A}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{swap}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\text{eval}_{\Omega}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{*}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes A}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda_{\mathbb{T}}^{(L)}\otimes A}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu\otimes A}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{swap}\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu\otimes\left[\mathbb{T}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\text{eval}_{\Omega}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\Phi^{\flat}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{eval}_{\Omega}}

Note that the the upper CCW path from

to

is the left adjoint of

, and the lower CW path between the same pair is the left adjoint of

.

We show the uniqueness of the factorization. Let

for some morphism

. Then, we have the following diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1_{\Context}\otimes A}

9.11  Proof of Theorem
13

Condition (i), which assumes that
\tilde{\omega}
is an enriched natural functor is equivalent to the commutation relation (
58
). The latter is simply (
41
) for the case
\mathcal{X}=\mathcal{T}
and
\mathcal{Y}=\Context
. Now by (
37
), (
41
) is equivalent to (
42
). Thus conditions (i), (ii) and (iii) are equivalent. We next show that this implies (iv). By Assumption
5
we have
\left[a\,;\,1\right]_{\text{int}}\cong 1
for every
a\in\Context
. We prove (
43
) by establishing two identities. Firstly :

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1_{\Context}\otimes\mathbb{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left[1_{\Context}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left[1_{\Context}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}\otimes\left[1_{\Context}\,;\,1_{\Context}\right]_{\text{int}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega_{*}\otimes!}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\circ}
right
adjoint
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1_{\Context}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes 1_{\Context}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes 1_{\Context}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1_{\Context}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces!}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega}

(72)

The dashed arrows in the two diagrams are thus left and right duals of each other. The next observation is

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1_{\Context}\otimes\mathbb{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes 1_{\Context}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left[1_{\Context}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left[\Omega\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}\otimes\left[1_{\Context}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda_{T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\rho_{T})^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi^{\sharp}\otimes\tilde{\omega}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\circ}
right
adjoint
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1_{\Context}\otimes 1_{\Context}\otimes\mathbb{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1_{\Context}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes 1_{\Context}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\Omega}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes 1_{\Context}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1_{\Context}\otimes\text{swap}_{1,T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda_{T}\otimes 1_{\Context}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\otimes\omega}

(73)

Before proving (
72
) and (
73
) we note how it proves the claim of the theorem. The composite morphisms in the left diagrams of (
72
) and (
73
) are the two commuting morphisms in (
42
). Thus they are equal. So their right adjoints are the same too. This means that the composite morphisms in the right diagrams of (
72
) and (
73
) are equal. But they are isomorphic to the two paths of (
43
), thus making them commute. It remains to prove (
72
) and (
73
). We prove them by proving commutation of their adjoints.

The adjoints of the two paths in (
72
) appear on the periphery of the following diagram :

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1_{\Context}\otimes\left[1_{\Context}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}\otimes\left[1_{\Context}\,;\,1_{\Context}\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1_{\Context}\otimes\mathbb{T}\otimes 1_{\Context}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1_{\Context}\otimes\left[1_{\Context}\,;\,1_{\Context}\right]_{\text{int}}\otimes\left[1_{\Context}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1_{\Context}\otimes\left[1_{\Context}\,;\,\Omega\right]_{\text{int}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1_{\Context}\otimes 1_{\Context}\otimes\left[1_{\Context}\,;\,1_{\Context}\right]_{\text{int}}}
